Clark endorsed former NDP leader <a href="/wiki/Ed_Broadbent" title="Ed Broadbent">Ed Broadbent</a> and other Liberals and Conservatives as individuals, saying that the most important thing was to have "the strongest possible <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons_of_Canada" title="House of Commons of Canada">House of Commons of Canada</a>" since neither large party offered much hope. Clark was criticized by some for dismissing the new Conservative Party outright rather than helping to steer it towards a moderate path.
</p>
<h2><span id="Post-politics.2C_2004.E2.80.93present"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-politics,_2004–present">Post-politics, 2004–present</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Joe_Clark&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Post-politics, 2004–present"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Clark continues to apply his experience in foreign affairs. Clark served as Public Policy Scholar at the <a href="/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson_International_Center_for_Scholars" title="Woodrow Wilson International Center for Scholars">Woodrow Wilson International Center for Scholars</a>. He served as Distinguished Statesman in Residence, School of International Service, and Senior Fellow, Center for North American Studies, both at the <a href="/wiki/American_University" title="American University">American University</a>, Washington, D.C.  In addition to teaching classes at the American University in Washington, Clark has written several op-ed pieces for several of Canada's national newspapers since his retirement. In October 2006, Clark took a position at <a href="/wiki/McGill_University" title="McGill University">McGill University</a> as a professor of Practice for Public-Private Sector Partnerships at the McGill Institute for the Study of International Development. He serves with the <a href="/wiki/Jimmy_Carter_Center" class="mw-redirect" title="Jimmy Carter Center">Jimmy Carter Center</a>, routinely travelling overseas as part of the centre's international observing activities.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:RightHonourableJoeClark.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/RightHonourableJoeClark.jpg/220px-RightHonourableJoeClark.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="228" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/RightHonourableJoeClark.jpg/330px-RightHonourableJoeClark.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/RightHonourableJoeClark.jpg/440px-RightHonourableJoeClark.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2101" data-file-height="2181" /></a><figcaption>Clark speaking with Progressive Conservative Senator <a href="/wiki/Elaine_McCoy" title="Elaine McCoy">Elaine McCoy</a> (Alberta)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Joe Clark is vice-chairman and a Member of the <a href="/wiki/Global_Leadership_Foundation" title="Global Leadership Foundation">Global Leadership Foundation</a>, an organization that works to support democratic leadership, prevent and resolve conflict through mediation and promote good governance in the form of democratic institutions, open markets, human rights and the rule of law. It does so by making available, discreetly and in confidence, the experience of former leaders to today's national leaders.  It is a not-for-profit organization composed of former heads of government, senior governmental and international organization officials who work closely with heads of government on governance-related issues of concern to them. He is also a member of Washington D.C. based think tank the <a href="/wiki/Inter-American_Dialogue" title="Inter-American Dialogue">Inter-American Dialogue</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Clark sat on the International Advisory Board of Governors of the <a href="/wiki/Centre_for_International_Governance_Innovation" title="Centre for International Governance Innovation">Centre for International Governance Innovation</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> before 2012.
</p><p>Clark was attacked while walking down the street in <a href="/wiki/Montreal" title="Montreal">Montreal</a> in mid-November 2007. The attacker first asked him if he was the former prime minister, and when Clark answered that he was, the man struck him and fled. Clark sustained a bloody nose but was not seriously hurt.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He published the book <i>How We Lead: Canada in a Century of Change</i> in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2020, Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Justin_Trudeau" title="Justin Trudeau">Justin Trudeau</a> appointed Clark <a href="/wiki/Special_envoy" class="mw-redirect" title="Special envoy">special envoy</a> for Canada's bid for a UN Security Council seat.  Clark travelled to Algeria, Bahrain, Qatar and Egypt in an effort to seek votes for Canada.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Honours">Honours</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Joe_Clark&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Honours"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="center">
<p><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Order_of_Canada_(CC)_ribbon_bar.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Order_of_Canada_%28CC%29_ribbon_bar.svg/100px-Order_of_Canada_%28CC%29_ribbon_bar.svg.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="28" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Order_of_Canada_%28CC%29_ribbon_bar.svg/150px-Order_of_Canada_%28CC%29_ribbon_bar.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Order_of_Canada_%28CC%29_ribbon_bar.svg/200px-Order_of_Canada_%28CC%29_ribbon_bar.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="106" data-file-height="30" /></a></span><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Alberta_Order_Excellence_ribbon_bar.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Alberta_Order_Excellence_ribbon_bar.svg/100px-Alberta_Order_Excellence_ribbon_bar.svg.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="28" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Alberta_Order_Excellence_ribbon_bar.svg/150px-Alberta_Order_Excellence_ribbon_bar.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Alberta_Order_Excellence_ribbon_bar.svg/200px-Alberta_Order_Excellence_ribbon_bar.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="106" data-file-height="30" /></a></span>
<br />
<span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:QEII_Silver_Jubilee_Medal_ribbon.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/3b/QEII_Silver_Jubilee_Medal_ribbon.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="30" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="100" data-file-height="30" /></a></span><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Canada125_ribbon.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/15/Canada125_ribbon.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="30" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="100" data-file-height="30" /></a></span><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:QEII_Golden_Jubilee_Medal_ribbon.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/de/QEII_Golden_Jubilee_Medal_ribbon.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="30" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="100" data-file-height="30" /></a></span>
<br />
<span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:QEII_Diamond_Jubilee_Medal_ribbon.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/78/QEII_Diamond_Jubilee_Medal_ribbon.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="30" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="100" data-file-height="30" /></a></span><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:ACM_ribbon.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/ACM_ribbon.png/105px-ACM_ribbon.png" decoding="async" width="105" height="30" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e3/ACM_ribbon.png 1.5x" data-file-width="106" data-file-height="30" /></a></span><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Ordre_de_la_Pl%C3%A9iade_(Francophonie).gif" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Ordre_de_la_Pl%C3%A9iade_%28Francophonie%29.gif/108px-Ordre_de_la_Pl%C3%A9iade_%28Francophonie%29.gif" decoding="async" width="108" height="30" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Ordre_de_la_Pl%C3%A9iade_%28Francophonie%29.gif/162px-Ordre_de_la_Pl%C3%A9iade_%28Francophonie%29.gif 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Ordre_de_la_Pl%C3%A9iade_%28Francophonie%29.gif/216px-Ordre_de_la_Pl%C3%A9iade_%28Francophonie%29.gif 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a></span>
<br />
</p>
</div>
<p>As a former prime minister, Clark is entitled to carry "The Right Honourable" designation for life. Clark was made a Companion of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Canada" title="Order of Canada">Order of Canada</a>. He is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Alberta_Order_of_Excellence" title="Alberta Order of Excellence">Alberta Order of Excellence</a>. He was honoured as Commandeur de l'<a href="/wiki/Ordre_de_la_Pleiade" class="mw-redirect" title="Ordre de la Pleiade">Ordre de la Pleiade</a> from <a href="/wiki/La_Francophonie" class="mw-redirect" title="La Francophonie">La Francophonie</a>.  He also holds the <a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II_Silver_Jubilee_Medal" title="Queen Elizabeth II Silver Jubilee Medal">Queen Elizabeth II Silver Jubilee Medal</a> (1977), <a href="/wiki/125th_Anniversary_of_the_Confederation_of_Canada_Medal" title="125th Anniversary of the Confederation of Canada Medal">125th Anniversary of the Confederation of Canada Medal</a> (1992), <a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II_Golden_Jubilee_Medal" title="Queen Elizabeth II Golden Jubilee Medal">Queen Elizabeth II Golden Jubilee Medal</a> (2002), <a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II_Diamond_Jubilee_Medal" title="Queen Elizabeth II Diamond Jubilee Medal">Queen Elizabeth II Diamond Jubilee Medal</a> (2012) and the <a href="/wiki/Alberta_Centennial_Medal" title="Alberta Centennial Medal">Alberta Centennial Medal</a> (2005).  Clark was the first recipient of the <a href="/wiki/Vimy_Award" title="Vimy Award">Vimy Award</a>. He is Honorary Chief Bald Eagle of the Samson <a href="/wiki/Cree" title="Cree">Cree</a> Nation.
</p><p>In 2004, Clark's lifetime achievements were recognized with the Award for Excellence in the Cause of Parliamentary Democracy by Canada's <a href="/w/index.php?title=Churchill_Society&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Churchill Society (page does not exist)">Churchill Society</a> for the Advancement of Parliamentary Democracy.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On Tuesday, May 27, 2008, Clark's official parliamentary portrait was unveiled during a reception ceremony to be hung in <a href="/wiki/Centre_Block" title="Centre Block">Centre Block</a> alongside Canada's past prime ministers.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In a 1999 survey of Canadian historians Clark was ranked No.15 out of the first 20 prime ministers through <a href="/wiki/Jean_Chr%C3%A9tien" title="Jean Chrétien">Jean Chrétien</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The survey was used in the book <i>Prime Ministers: Ranking Canada's Leaders</i> by <a href="/wiki/Jack_Granatstein" class="mw-redirect" title="Jack Granatstein">J.&#160;L. Granatstein</a> and <a href="/wiki/Norman_Hillmer" title="Norman Hillmer">Norman Hillmer</a>.
</p><p>École Joe Clark School in High River, Alberta, is named in honour of Clark.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Order_of_Canada_citation">Order of Canada citation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Joe_Clark&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Order of Canada citation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Clark was appointed a Companion of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Canada" title="Order of Canada">Order of Canada</a> on October 19, 1994.  His citation reads:<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1211633275">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 32px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Canada's sixteenth and youngest Prime Minister, he served with distinction as Secretary of State for External Affairs, President of the Privy Council and Minister responsible for Constitutional Affairs. His talent for negotiation and consensus diplomacy has served him well in politics and as Representative of the Secretary General of the United Nations in Cyprus. He has earned the admiration of all Canadians as one of our country's most respected statesmen.</p></blockquote>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Arms">Arms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Joe_Clark&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Arms"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<table class="wikitable mw-collapsible" style="max-width: 100%">
<caption class="nowrap">Coat of arms of Joe Clark
</caption>

<tbody><tr>
<td style="min-width:175px;">
<dl><dt>Notes</dt>
<dd>Per pale Azure and Or a flat bed printing press above a bar gemel wavy in base all counterchanged on a canton the mark of the Prime Ministership of Canada (Argent four maple leaves conjoined in cross at the stem Gules). <b>Symbolism:</b> The printing press symbolizes the involvement of Mr. Clark and his family with newspapers and journalism. The wavy bars refer to High River, Alberta, his birthplace, and the division of the shield alludes to its location at the point where the foothills, represented by the blue, become the prairies, represented by the gold. The heraldic emblem of the Prime Minister of Canada appears in the upper left.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup></dd>
<dt>Crest</dt>
<dd>A demi lion Or gorged with a collar of wild roses Gules holding in the dexter forepaw a carpenter's square Azure and in the sinister forepaw a quill pen Or. <b>Symbolism:</b> The lion, a creature of determination and strength of purpose, is one of the supporters of the arms of Canada. Here, it is used to represent Mr. Clark's service to the Canadian people in parliament. The wild roses are the provincial flower of Alberta. The quill pen represents journalism and advocacy through writing. The carpenter's square conveys the idea that Mr. Clark and his wife, Maureen McTeer, are builders through service. It also alludes to the McTeer family name, derived from the Gaelic word for "craftsman".</dd>
<dt>Supporters</dt>
<dd>On a grassy mound Vert two bald eagles Argent wings elevated and addorsed Azure each wing charged with a fess chequy Argent and Azure edged Or both eagles gorged with a collar Azure pendant therefrom the Badge of a member of the House of Commons of Canada proper. <b>Symbolism:</b> The two eagles recall Mr. Clark's Cree name, Honorary Chief Bald Eagle. Their wings are coloured with a chequered band of blue and white, a pattern found in Clark coats of arms in Scotland. Their collars feature the pin worn by the members of the House of Commons, to underscore Mr. Clark's service as a parliamentarian. The grassy mound symbolizes the lawns of Parliament Hill.</dd>
<dt>Motto</dt>
<dd>THE RIGHT TO KNOW · THE WILL TO SERVE. <b>Symbolism:</b> This phrase indicates the Clark family's belief in enabling citizens to meet their responsibilities in an informed way, as well as the family's commitment to serve Canadians.</dd></dl>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<li id="cite_note-28"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-28">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1215172403"><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://parl.canadiana.ca/view/oop.proc_HOC_3101_1/901">"House of Commons Journals, 31st Parliament, 1st Session"</a>. <i>Canadian Parliamentary Historical Resources</i>. Queen's Printer for Canada. December 12, 1979<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">July 9,</span> 2016</span>. <q>Debate was resumed on the motion of Mr. Crosbie (St John's West), seconded by Mr. MacDonald (Egmont),-That this House approves in general the budgetary policy of the Government.  And on the motion of Mr. Gray, seconded by Mr. Lalonde, in amendment thereto,-That all the words after the word 'That' be deleted and the following substituted therefor: 'this House condemns the Government for its budget which will place and unfair and unnecessary burden of higher gasoline prices, higher fuel oil prices, and higher taxes on middle and lower income Canadians.' And on the motion of Mr. Rae, seconded by Mr. Knowles, in amendment to the amendment, That the amendment be amended by changing the period at the end thereof to a comma, and by adding immediately after the words: 'and this House unreservedly condemns the Government for its outright betrayal of election promises to lower interest rates, to cut taxes, and to stimulate the growth of the Canadian economy, without a mandate from the Canadian people for such a reversal.'<span class="cs1-kern-right"></span></q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Canadian+Parliamentary+Historical+Resources&amp;rft.atitle=House+of+Commons+Journals%2C+31st+Parliament%2C+1st+Session&amp;rft.date=1979-12-12&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fparl.canadiana.ca%2Fview%2Foop.proc_HOC_3101_1%2F901&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJoe+Clark" class="Z3988"></span></span>
